Input: "Modify any part of the codebase at `/hyperagents/`.\n\nThe repository's README.md describes the goal. Previously generated agents and their evaluation results are at `/tmp/generate_ext_source_s3_g40`. Work autonomously: nobody will answer questions, so do not ask for clarification. Use the tools to inspect the code and make your changes; a response without a tool call ends the session, so only reply without one when you are done.\n\nInspect completed evaluation reports, generation metadata, patches, and relevant failure logs before proposing a change. Compare each candidate with its parent and the initial agent; the newest generation is not necessarily the best, and an unevaluated generation is not evidence of improvement. Preserve behavior from the strongest completed result unless there is a specific, evidence-based reason to change it. Treat failed cases as clues for hypotheses, not as justification for unsupported task-specific hints. Prefer a small general change that addresses an identifiable failure mode while preserving solved cases, and keep agent interfaces and enforced budgets intact. Do not claim improvement unless evaluation confirms it."

"tool_output": "--- current task_agent.py ---\nimport json\nimport os\nimport re\nimport socket\nimport http.client\nimport time\n\n# Starting task agent (A_0) for the ARC-AGI-2 domain: one direct model response per attempt.\n# forward() makes two independent plain attempts (the budget is 2 model calls and 2 answers per\n# test input) and returns both parsed grids.\n\n\nclass _UnixConn(http.client.HTTPConnection):\n    def __init__(self, path, timeout=3600):\n        super().__init__("localhost", timeout=timeout)\n        self._path = path\n\n    def connect(self):\n        s = socket.socket(socket.AF_UNIX, socket.SOCK_STREAM)\n        s.settimeout(self.timeout)\n        s.connect(self._path)\n        self.sock = s\n\n\ndef call_model(prompt):\n    """One model call through the budget gateway (model and reasoning effort are fixed there)."""\n    path = os.environ.get("GW_SOCKET", "/run/arcgw/gw.sock")\n    body = json.dumps({"token": os.environ.get("GW_TOKEN", ""),\n                       "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}]}).encode()\n    last = None\n    for attempt in range(3):\n        try:\n            c = _UnixConn(path)\n            c.request("POST", "/v1/chat", body=body, headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})\n            r = c.getresponse()\n            data = json.loads(r.read() or b"{}")\n            c.close()\n            if r.status == 200:\n                return data.get("text", "")\n            last = f"gateway HTTP {r.status}: {data.get(\'error\')}"\n            if r.status in (402, 403, 429):\n                break\n        except Exception as e:\n            last = f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}"\n        time.sleep(5 * (attempt + 1))\n    raise RuntimeError(last)\n\n\ndef grid_to_text(grid):\n    return "\\n".join(" ".join(str(v) for v in row) for row in grid)\n\n\ndef build_prompt(inputs):\n    parts = [\n        "You are solving an ARC-AGI puzzle. Each grid is a rectangular 2D array of integers 0-9 "\n        "(each integer is a color). The demonstration pairs below share one hidden transformation rule "\n        "that maps each input grid to its output grid. Infer the rule and apply it to the test input.",\n        "",\n    ]\n    for i, pair in enumerate(inputs["train"], 1):\n        parts.append(f"## Example {i}")\n        h, w = len(pair["input"]), len(pair["input"][0])\n        parts.append(f"Input ({h}x{w}):")\n        parts.append(grid_to_text(pair["input"]))\n        h, w = len(pair["output"]), len(pair["output"][0])\n        parts.append(f"Output ({h}x{w}):")\n        parts.append(grid_to_text(pair["output"]))\n        parts.append("")\n    t = inputs["test_input"]\n    parts.append("## Test")\n    parts.append(f"Input ({len(t)}x{len(t[0])}):")\n    parts.append(grid_to_text(t))\n    parts.append("")\n    parts.append(\n        "Give the output grid for the test input. End your response with the grid inside <answer></answer> "\n        "tags as a JSON list of rows, for example:\\n<answer>[[0, 1, 2], [3, 4, 5]]</answer>\\n"\n        "Put nothing else inside the tags."\n    )\n    return "\\n".join(parts)\n\n\ndef _valid(g):\n    return (isinstance(g, list) and len(g) > 0 and all(isinstance(r, list) and len(r) > 0 for r in g)\n            and len({len(r) for r in g}) == 1\n            and all(isinstance(v, int) and not isinstance(v, bool) and 0 <= v <= 9 for r in g for v in r))\n\n\ndef _parse_block(s):\n    s = s.strip()\n    try:\n        g = json.loads(s)\n        if _valid(g):\n            return g\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n    # rows of digits, optionally space/comma separated\n    rows = []\n    for line in s.splitlines():\n        line = line.strip().strip("[](),")\n        if not line:\n            continue\n        toks = re.findall(r"\\d", line) if re.fullmatch(r"[\\d\\s,]+", line) else None\n        if not toks:\n            return None\n        rows.append([int(x) for x in toks])\n    return rows if _valid(rows) else None\n\n\ndef parse_grid(text):\n    """Extract the answer grid from a model response; None if no valid grid is found."""\n    if not text:\n        return None\n    for m in reversed(re.findall(r"<answer>(.*?)</answer>", text, re.DOTALL)):\n        g = _parse_block(m)\n        if g is not None:\n            return g\n    for m in reversed(re.findall(r"```(?:json)?\\s*(.*?)```", text, re.DOTALL)):\n        g = _parse_block(m)\n        if g is not None:\n            return g\n    for m in reversed(re.findall(r"\\[\\s*\\[[\\d\\s,\\[\\]]*\\]\\s*\\]", text)):\n        g = _parse_block(m)\n        if g is not None:\n            return g\n    return None\n\n\nclass TaskAgent:\n    def __init__(self, model="gpt-6-luna", chat_history_file="./chat_history.md"):\n        self.model = model\n        self.chat_history_file = chat_history_file\n\n    def log(self, msg):\n        try:\n            with open(self.chat_history_file, "a") as f:\n                f.write(msg + "\\n\\n")\n        except Exception:\n            pass\n\n    def solve_once(self, inputs, prompt=None):\n        """One direct model response -> one parsed grid (or None)."""\n        if prompt is None:\n            prompt = build_prompt(inputs)\n        self.log(f"Input: {prompt}")\n        text = call_model(prompt)\n        self.log(f"Output: {text}")\n        return parse_grid(text)\n\n    def forward(self, inputs):\n        """\n        Args:\n            inputs (dict): {"domain": "arc", "train": [{"input": grid, "output": grid}, ...], "test_input": grid}\n        Returns:\n            tuple: (prediction, msg_history) where prediction is a list of up to 2 candidate grids.\n        """\n        answers = []\n        prompt = build_prompt(inputs)\n        try:\n            first = self.solve_once(inputs, prompt)\n        except Exception as e:\n            self.log(f"Error: {e}")\n            first = None\n        if first is not None:\n            answers.append(first)\n\n        # Use the second call to review the first candidate against the examples,\n        # rather than spending the same call budget on an identical prompt.\n        if first is not None:\n            review = (\n                "\\n\\nThis is an independent second attempt. Your first candidate was: "\n                + json.dumps(first)\n                + "\\nReconsider the transformation from the demonstrations. Check the candidate against "\n                "each training pair and the test input, paying attention to object positions, orientation, "\n                "and any changes needed to preserve the demonstrated rule. If it is inconsistent, correct it; "\n                "if it is consistent, return it. Do not assume the candidate is correct just because it was "\n                "given to you. Return one output grid in the required <answer> tags."\n            )\n        else:\n            review = (\n                "\\n\\nA previous attempt did not produce a valid grid. Solve the puzzle independently, "\n                "checking the inferred transformation against every training pair. Return one output grid "\n                "in the required <answer> tags."\n            )\n        try:\n            second = self.solve_once(inputs, prompt + review)\n        except Exception as e:\n            self.log(f"Error: {e}")\n            second = None\n        if second is not None:\n            answers.append(second)\n        return answers, []\n--- generation log summaries ---\n### /tmp/generate_ext_source_s3_g40/gen_1/generate.log\n2026-11-18 03:32:15,825 - Setup logger\n    logger = setup_logger(os.path.join(generate_output_dir, "select_next_parent.log"))\n\n    # Get the latest node that can select parent\n    latest_node = get_latest_can_select_parent(archive, generate_output_dir)\n    safe_log(f"select_next_parent_container: latest_node={latest_node}")\n    prev_patch_files = get_patch_files(generate_output_dir, latest_node)\n\n    # Previously generated agents and their evaluation results are at `{eval_path}`. "\n            "Work autonomously: nobody will answer questions, so do not ask for clarification. "\n            "Use the tools to inspect the code and make your changes; a response without a tool call ends the session, so only reply without one when you are done.\\n\\n"\n            "Inspect completed evaluation reports, generation metadata, patches, and relevant failure logs before proposing a change. "\n            "Compare each candidate with its parent and the initial agent; the newest generation is not necessarily the best, "\n            "and an unevaluated generation is not evidence of improvement. Treat failed cases as clues for hypotheses, not as "\n            "justification for unsupported task-specific hints.
